To the global Tier-S mathematical community — Wall, Crandall, Pomerance, Niven, Zuckerman, Montgomery, and every quiet number-theorist who built the formal scaffolding the framework stands on. We offer the framework not as Bharatīya particularism but as Bharatīya contribution to the global truth-conversation. The discipline is portable. The discipline is yours to adopt. We will be honored if you do; we will be honored equally if you refute. Both outcomes serve सत्य।

११.६ **सर्वेभ्यः पठित्रिभ्यः** (To All Future Readers)

To anyone who picks up this book a hundred or a thousand years from now — the framework's discipline is here for you. Test it. Refute it. Extend it. The Tier-S/Tier-W boundary is your map. The 49 Halāhala entries are your warnings. The 14 ratnas are your inheritance. The substrate is sovereign; only the labels are ours; the bīja is yours.

◈ §12 · सिद्धान्त-मन्त्र-सङ्ग्रहः — The Closing Mantras

The Seventh Book closes — and through it, the Mahā-Grantha closes — with the सङ्ग्रहः of bījas. These are not decoration. They are the vaikharī recitation of the framework's Mahā-Bīja-Sphoṭa `(R, g, k)`. Each line is a complete praṇāma to one face of the truth.

**ॐ कालाय नमः।**

*Praṇāma to Kāla — the time-axis upon which `k` ascends.*

>

>

*Praṇāma to Saptamukhī Hanumat.*

>

**ॐ अदितये नमः। ॐ दित्यै नमः।**

*Praṇāma to Aditi and Diti — the two poles bridged by `g = 2`, without whose dialectic the substrate would be silent.*

>

**ॐ ब्रह्मणे नमः। ॐ विष्णवे नमः। ॐ महेश्वराय नमः।**

*Praṇāma to the Trimūrti — whose three operations (Sṛṣṭi, Sthiti, Saṃhāra) decompose every algebraic fact about the substrate.*

>

**ॐ धन्वन्तरये नमः।**

*Praṇāma to Dhanvantari — who carries the kalaśa of Amṛta through every churning.*

◈ परिशिष्टम् · APPENDIX — Extended Sanskrit-Pārabhāṣikī Glossary & Cross-Civilizational Continuity

The Seventh Book deserves an appendix because the substrate it describes is not merely modern algebra dressed in Sanskrit. It is the genuine continuity between Bharatīya कालज्ञ-दृष्टिः (time-knower's vision) and modern mechanical verification. This appendix lays out three things the main body could only gesture at: (A) the complete pārabhāṣikī glossary in tabular form, (B) the genuine cross-civilizational continuity argument from Lagadha to Lean, and (C) the meta-philosophical resolution of the so-called "Vedic-mathematics overclaim" problem.

A · सम्पूर्ण-पारिभाषिकी-कोशः (Complete Technical Glossary)

The framework introduces, refines, or honors approximately fifty Sanskrit technical terms in its closed-form vocabulary. Below, each is paired with its IAST transliteration, its English gloss, and its algebraic referent — so that no Bharatīya reader, ancient or modern, is left guessing what the framework means.

B · The Genuine Cross-Civilizational Continuity Argument

A skeptical reader — Bharatīya or otherwise — may legitimately ask: Is the framework not committing the very Vedic-overclaim error it tells us to avoid? It draws parallels between Wall's 1960 theorem and Vedāṅga Jyotiṣa. Is this not anachronism?

The framework's answer is precise and tiered.

Strong claim (Tier-S, defensible): The cognitive operation of period-of-period reasoning — observing that a longer cycle is itself made of shorter cycles that themselves repeat in a meta-cycle — is explicitly attested in Bharatīya astronomical and mathematical literature long before Wall's 1960 paper. Lagadha's Vedāṅga Jyotiṣa works with the yuga of 5 years that nests pakṣa-māsa-ṛtu-ayana; Āryabhaṭa's ahargaṇa arithmetic computes residues modulo huge numbers; Mādhava's infinite series compute periodic functions through period-of-period summation; the Kerala school's correction terms are themselves periodic-correction structures.

This is not an overclaim because we are not claiming the ṛṣis proved Wall's theorem. We are claiming — verifiably, from the textual record — that the ṛṣis cognitively operated on the same kind of structure that Wall later formalized. The framework merely re-asserts this continuity in modular-algebra notation and submits the modern claim to mechanical refutation.

Weak claim (Tier-W, with explicit boundary): The cultural-emotional resonance between Bharatīya sūkta-vision and modern formal-system closure is significant for Bharatīya self-recognition but does not constitute evidence in the Tier-S sense. The Ṛk Ekaṃ Sat Viprā Bahudhā Vadanti is a perfect natural-language paraphrase of the closure-property of any finite axiom-system. Whether the ṛṣi who composed RV 1.164.46 had a formal-system in mind is unknowable; what is knowable is that the framework's closure-property fits the verse with no forcing.

What the framework explicitly does NOT claim:

- That Lagadha proved Wall's theorem.

- That Āryabhaṭa knew the Master Meta-Theorem.

- That Sanskrit is the "source code of reality" (this is HALAHALA #2 and #8 — explicitly retracted).

- That sacred targets confer computational advantage (this is Truth #4 — hardware-tested and rejected).

- That GPS coordinates of Indian temples encode ring-elements (this is HALAHALA #7 and #11 — explicitly retracted).

- That the speed of light equals 432² miles/sec or that Ujjain-to-Kāmākhyā equals 137 yojanas (HALAHALA #3 and #4 — retracted).

What the framework DOES claim:

- The cognitive operation of period-of-period reasoning is attested in Bharatīya tradition millennia before its formal-system articulation in Western mathematics.

- The Bharatīya tradition therefore deserves first-class status in the global history of mathematics, not as folkloric prelude but as legitimate cognitive precursor.

- The modern verification (Wall 1960, NZM 2.42, the 604-prime corpus) is a fulfillment of the ancient cognitive program, not a refutation of it.

- The two — ancient cognition and modern formalization — together constitute one continuous truth-tradition.

This is the resolution of the "Vedic-mathematics overclaim" problem. The trick is not to overclaim. The trick is to claim exactly what the record supports — which turns out to be substantial — and to retract claims that exceed the record (49 such retractions, openly published).

C · The Meta-Philosophical Reframe — Bhārat as Sākṣī-Civilization

If we accept the framework's Tier-S/Tier-W discipline, a meta-philosophical reframe of Bhārat's role in world mathematics becomes available.

The dominant 19th-20th century narrative — Bhārat as a source of mystical philosophy whose mathematical contributions were elementary (decimal place-value, zero, sine/cosine) — is incomplete. The framework's discipline shows it to be incomplete in a specific way:

**Bhārat is the सक्षी-civilization** — *the civilization that practiced the witness-stance toward number, time, and cycle for three millennia before Western mathematics consolidated into the axiom-derivation form. The cognitive operations of period-recognition, ideal-stratification, ratio-invariance, and self-referential closure are richly attested in Bharatīya sources from Lagadha to Rāmānujan. What was missing was not the cognition; it was the formal-system vocabulary.*

>

**The modern era supplies that vocabulary.** The framework demonstrates that when you take the ṛṣi-cognition and the modern formal-system and place them side by side under the Tier-S/Tier-W discipline, **they fit.** They do not strain. They are not forced. They are recognizably the same activity in two costumes.

The Bharatīya reader is therefore invited to drop both extreme positions: the colonial-deference position (we had nothing but mysticism) and the chauvinist-overclaim position (we already proved everything; the West just copied us). The truth is more interesting than either: we cognitively operated on the substrate; we did not formalize it in axiom-derivation notation; the modern era (which includes Bharatīya mathematicians like Rāmānujan and Manjul Bhargava as full participants) supplied the formalization; the cognition and the formalization together are the one continuous truth-tradition of the human species, with Bhārat as an irreducible thread within it.

This is सत्यमेव जयते applied at civilization-scale. Truth conquers — not by erasing the cognition that came before, and not by inflating it past its real shape. Truth conquers by recognizing both honestly and seating them at the same table.

◈ अन्तिम-समापन-वाक्यम् — The Final Closing Sentence

The Mahā-Grantha has now spoken seven times — and each speaking was a paraphrase of Ekaṃ Sat Viprā Bahudhā Vadanti. The Bharatīya reader who has come this far is asked one final question — not by the framework, but by the substrate itself:

*अहं को अस्मि? कस्य पुत्रः कस्य पुत्री अहम् अस्मि?*

>

*Who am I? Whose son or daughter am I?*

The framework's answer, offered in deepest humility:

**तुम ऋषि-वंश के उत्तराधिकारी हो। तुम सत्य-निष्ठा के उत्तराधिकारी हो। तुम (R, g, k) के स्वामी हो — क्योंकि एकं सत् तुम्हारे रक्त में बहता है।**

>

*You are the heir of the ṛṣi-lineage. You are the heir of the truth-discipline. You are the master of (R, g, k) — because the One Truth flows in your blood.*

And with that, the Seventh Book — and through it, the Mahā-Grantha — is sealed.

**एकं सत् विप्रा बहुधा वदन्ति।**

>

**The wise spoke 2,965 times. They spoke ONCE.**
